03Refill requests and response windowsA status call is not a promise of supply+

Your goal

Explain an unresolved renewal and make a reliable communication commitment without promising prescriber response or medicine availability.

This lesson in the book, page 22 ↗

A Read the situation

A status call is not a promise of supply

At 11:00, patient Casey asks technician Jo about a refill. The record shows no refills remaining. A renewal request was sent to the prescriber at 09:00, but no response has arrived. Jo can call Casey between 15:00 and 16:00 with a status update. No prescriber response time, supply, or collection time is confirmed. Jo needs to explain what was sent, what remains pending, and what the call will cover. Any concern about running out or interrupted treatment should be referred promptly to the pharmacist rather than treated as safe to wait for the update.

Casey: Patient / Jo: Pharmacy technician

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What does the refill record show?

Answer and explanation

C. No refills remaining

The available record has no remaining refills and the renewal response is pending.

2. When was the renewal request sent?

Answer and explanation

C. 09:00

The brief gives 09:00 as the sent time and 11:00 as the current inquiry time.

3. What can Jo commit to?

Answer and explanation

C. A status call between 15:00 and 16:00

Jo controls the stated update call, not the prescriber's response or supply outcome.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

A transmission confirmation may show that the request left the system or reached a destination. It is not a prescriber approval. Similarly, a status call between 15:00 and 16:00 is a communication commitment, not a guaranteed collection window.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
refill
A further supply under an existing prescription when permitted and available.request a refill
refills remaining
The number of further supplies recorded as authorized under the prescription.check refills remaining
renewal
A request or authorization for a further prescription when the existing authorization is insufficient.request a prescription renewal
renewal request
A message asking the prescriber to authorize the relevant further prescription.send a renewal request
prescriber response
The actual reply from the prescribing professional or practice.await the prescriber response
authorization pending
A status in which the required approval has not yet been received.explain authorization pending
request timestamp
The time recorded for sending or receiving the request.verify the request timestamp
transmission confirmation
Evidence that a message was sent or delivered through a system, not that it was approved.check transmission confirmation
response window
A stated period for an expected or promised response whose status must be clear.qualify the response window
callback window
The agreed period in which a return call will be made.confirm the callback window
status call
A call providing current information rather than guaranteeing an outcome.make a status call
collection time
The time at which an order is confirmed available for pickup.confirm collection time
supply authorization
The permission needed for the particular medicine supply under applicable rules.verify supply authorization
partial fill
Supply of less than the prescribed quantity where permitted under applicable arrangements.refer a partial-fill question
emergency supply
A medicine supply considered under specific legal and clinical requirements.refer an emergency-supply question
prescription transfer
Movement of prescription information between pharmacies where permitted.clarify prescription-transfer requirements
refill too soon
A processing response indicating that the requested supply is earlier than the applicable rules allow.explain a refill-too-soon response
remaining quantity
The amount still available or authorized under the relevant record.verify the remaining quantity
adherence
Taking medicine in accordance with the agreed clinical instructions.discuss an adherence concern
treatment interruption
A break in the prescribed treatment that may require clinical advice.report a treatment interruption
continuity of therapy
Maintaining appropriate treatment under qualified guidance.support continuity of therapy
follow-up owner
The person responsible for the specified next contact or action.name the follow-up owner
contact outcome
Whether and how a communication attempt reached the intended person.record the contact outcome
pending response
A reply still awaited rather than received.track a pending response

No refills remaining does not authorize the technician to invent a supply or advise the person to interrupt treatment. Questions about immediate need, emergency supply, partial fills, or alternatives require the pharmacist and applicable rules; the examples confer no entitlement.

C Notice the language

Give certainty about the action you control

Patients often hear we will call as it will be ready. Name the purpose of the call and repeat the distinction without sounding evasive. A clear update commitment can be useful even when the pharmacy cannot promise another professional's response.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
State the record
The record shows no refills remaining.
Give the sent time
We sent the renewal request at nine this morning.
State the current time
As of eleven, no response has arrived.
Keep stages separate
Sending the request does not mean the renewal is approved.
Own the follow-up
I will call you with the status between three and four.
Explain the call
That is an update window, not a confirmed collection time.
Avoid a prescriber promise
I cannot guarantee when the prescriber will respond.
Keep supply unconfirmed
No further supply has been confirmed yet.
Route immediate need
Please discuss any concern about running out with the pharmacist now.
Do not advise interruption
I cannot tell you to stop or change treatment while waiting.
Clarify the update content
I will tell you whether a response has arrived and what remains pending.
Preserve an unsuccessful attempt
If I cannot reach you, I will record the attempt through our process.
Verify contact
Let us confirm the appropriate contact details.
Avoid assuming denial
No response is not the same as a refusal.
Identify the next owner
I am responsible for the agreed status call.
Close accurately
The request is pending, and I will update you even if it is still unresolved.
6 language and precision notes
As of eleven
Establishes the time boundary of the reported status.
Does not mean approved
Separates transmission from authorization.
With the status
Specifies the call's purpose instead of implying readiness.
Even if unresolved
Makes the update commitment useful without guaranteeing an external result.
No response versus refusal
Distinguishes silence from a negative decision.
Now
Keeps a current clinical need separate from a later routine update.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which update is accurate at 11:00?

Answer and explanation

A. The 09:00 renewal request is unanswered; Jo will give a status update between 15:00 and 16:00.

The accurate version preserves the request time, unresolved response, and separate communication commitment.

2. What does a successful transmission not prove?

Answer and explanation

D. Prescriber authorization

Transmission records communication activity rather than the clinical or legal authorization sought.

3. What should happen to a concern about running out?

Answer and explanation

B. Refer it promptly to the pharmacist for appropriate assessment.

The pharmacist must address the actual need and applicable options; the status-call window is not clinical advice.

4. Who owns the promised call?

Answer and explanation

A. Jo

Jo explicitly commits to the status call and should record its actual contact outcome.

Check explanations in the book, page 89 ↗

E Conversations

A status call is not a promise of supply

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Casey: Patient / Jo: Pharmacy technician

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • pending response
  • callback window
  • status call
  • follow-up owner
  • contact outcome
  • emergency supply
  • Transmission confirmation
  • refills remaining
  • treatment interruption
  • request timestamp
  1. Casey
    Jo, I asked for a refill earlier. It is eleven now, and I would like to know whether I can come and collect it this afternoon.
  2. Jo
    The record shows no 1. We sent a renewal request at nine, but no response has arrived as of eleven, so I cannot confirm collection yet.
  3. Casey
    Does that mean the prescriber refused it? I am worried that no reply means they have decided I should not have it anymore.
  4. Jo
    A 2 is not the same as a refusal. I can report what the record shows, but I should not invent a decision or a reason for the delay.
  5. Casey
    Can you check that it actually went out? Last time, requested meant someone had put it on a list to send later.
  6. Jo
    The 3 is nine this morning. We should distinguish that sent event from a task waiting to be sent, while also keeping it separate from a completed authorization.
  7. Casey
    If the system says the message went through, why can that not count as approval? It seems as though the practice must have received it.
  8. Jo
    4 concerns the message, not the decision. Even when delivery is confirmed, the actual response and any remaining pharmacy checks still need to be established.
  9. Casey
    What can you tell me about this afternoon? I do not want to travel here on the assumption that a phone call means the order is ready.
  10. Jo
    I can promise a 5 between three and four. I will tell you whether a response has arrived and what remains pending; that is not a guaranteed collection time.
  11. Casey
    So if there is still no answer by then, you will call anyway rather than wait silently until the order is eventually finished?
  12. Jo
    Yes. I own that 6. The update should happen within it even if the status is unchanged, so you know what has and has not been confirmed.
  13. Casey
    I am worried about running out before anyone replies. Can I speak to the pharmacist now, rather than wait for the afternoon call?
  14. Jo
    Please discuss that possible 7 with the pharmacist now. I cannot advise you to stop, change, or wait without the appropriate clinical guidance.
  15. Casey
    Could the pharmacist discuss whether any immediate supply route is possible? I understand you cannot promise one just because I ask.
  16. Jo
    The pharmacist can assess the actual need and rules around any 8 or other appropriate option. I cannot authorize that independently or guarantee that a particular route applies.
  17. Casey
    Please use my verified contact details for the update. If you cannot reach me, I would not want the note to say I was fully informed.
  18. Jo
    I will record the actual 9. A call attempt is not the same as speaking with you, and any further action must follow our communication process.
  19. Casey
    Thank you. I understand the renewal is still pending, no supply time is confirmed, and you will update me between three and four.
  20. Jo
    Correct. I am the 10 for that call. We will keep your immediate concern with the pharmacist and keep the later update separate from any promise of prescriber response or medicine availability.

Answers and explanations
  1. refills remaining Turn 2. Refills remaining describes existing authorization in the record; zero means the requested further supply is not already authorized there.
  2. pending response Turn 4. A pending response means no reply has arrived; it does not establish a refusal or a change in treatment.
  3. request timestamp Turn 6. The request timestamp records nine o'clock as the sending time and separates an actual sent request from a planned action.
  4. Transmission confirmation Turn 8. Transmission confirmation can establish message movement but does not establish that the prescriber reviewed and approved the renewal.
  5. status call Turn 10. The status call provides current information and does not guarantee that the order will be ready for collection.
  6. callback window Turn 12. The callback window is Jo's agreed three-to-four communication period, independent of whether the renewal has been resolved.
  7. treatment interruption Turn 14. Treatment interruption can raise a clinical concern that needs prompt pharmacist assessment rather than a routine update assumption.
  8. emergency supply Turn 16. Emergency supply depends on the applicable clinical and legal requirements and is not automatically available from a technician's promise.
  9. contact outcome Turn 18. Contact outcome distinguishes a successful conversation from an attempt or message, so the record does not falsely imply the patient was informed.
  10. follow-up owner Turn 20. The follow-up owner is Jo for the agreed status call, not the person who can guarantee the prescriber's response.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Casey: Jo, I asked for a refill earlier. It is eleven now, and I would like to know whether I can come and collect it this afternoon.
  2. Jo: The record shows no refills remaining. We sent a renewal request at nine, but no response has arrived as of eleven, so I cannot confirm collection yet.
  3. Casey: Does that mean the prescriber refused it? I am worried that no reply means they have decided I should not have it anymore.
  4. Jo: A pending response is not the same as a refusal. I can report what the record shows, but I should not invent a decision or a reason for the delay.
  5. Casey: Can you check that it actually went out? Last time, requested meant someone had put it on a list to send later.
  6. Jo: The request timestamp is nine this morning. We should distinguish that sent event from a task waiting to be sent, while also keeping it separate from a completed authorization.
  7. Casey: If the system says the message went through, why can that not count as approval? It seems as though the practice must have received it.
  8. Jo: Transmission confirmation concerns the message, not the decision. Even when delivery is confirmed, the actual response and any remaining pharmacy checks still need to be established.
  9. Casey: What can you tell me about this afternoon? I do not want to travel here on the assumption that a phone call means the order is ready.
  10. Jo: I can promise a status call between three and four. I will tell you whether a response has arrived and what remains pending; that is not a guaranteed collection time.
  11. Casey: So if there is still no answer by then, you will call anyway rather than wait silently until the order is eventually finished?
  12. Jo: Yes. I own that callback window. The update should happen within it even if the status is unchanged, so you know what has and has not been confirmed.
  13. Casey: I am worried about running out before anyone replies. Can I speak to the pharmacist now, rather than wait for the afternoon call?
  14. Jo: Please discuss that possible treatment interruption with the pharmacist now. I cannot advise you to stop, change, or wait without the appropriate clinical guidance.
  15. Casey: Could the pharmacist discuss whether any immediate supply route is possible? I understand you cannot promise one just because I ask.
  16. Jo: The pharmacist can assess the actual need and rules around any emergency supply or other appropriate option. I cannot authorize that independently or guarantee that a particular route applies.
  17. Casey: Please use my verified contact details for the update. If you cannot reach me, I would not want the note to say I was fully informed.
  18. Jo: I will record the actual contact outcome. A call attempt is not the same as speaking with you, and any further action must follow our communication process.
  19. Casey: Thank you. I understand the renewal is still pending, no supply time is confirmed, and you will update me between three and four.
  20. Jo: Correct. I am the follow-up owner for that call. We will keep your immediate concern with the pharmacist and keep the later update separate from any promise of prescriber response or medicine availability.
Dialogue in the book, page 28 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

The nine o'clock request

Fictional refill call using the lesson case.

  1. Patient: I requested a refill this morning. Has my prescriber answered, or is it ready?
  2. Technician: There are no refills remaining. We sent a renewal request at nine; no response is recorded.
  3. Patient: It's eleven now. Does sending the request mean the prescriber has accepted it?
  4. Technician: No. Sending it confirms our action, not the prescriber's answer or permission for another supply.
  5. Patient: I have to leave home at four. Could someone call before I go?
  6. Technician: Jo's callback window is three to four. She'll report the status even if no answer arrives.
  7. Patient: Please don't leave me expecting a pickup message if the request is still pending.
  8. Technician: We'll call it a renewal update. Any collection readiness would need separate confirmation.
  9. Patient: What should I do about a medication question while I wait for that response?
  10. Technician: I can connect you to the pharmacist for that question now; the renewal status remains pending.
Two requests, one record

Fictional duplicate web and telephone refill requests; both refer to the same prescription.

  1. Technician: I see a web request and your telephone message for the same prescription number.
  2. Patient: I submitted the second one because I wasn't sure the website had worked this morning.
  3. Technician: Thank you for explaining. Are both requests for the same supply, rather than separate prescriptions?
  4. Patient: Yes, the number is the same. I don't want two orders or two charges.
  5. Technician: I'll flag the duplicate request for our queue review; I haven't confirmed two supplies or charges.
  6. Patient: Does that mean I need to cancel the original request and start from the beginning?
  7. Technician: No, we're checking the duplicate against the original. I won't ask you to submit another.
  8. Patient: The website still says received. Can you tell me which message I should follow?
  9. Technician: Use the update for this prescription number. I'll note that the second contact was a duplicate.
  10. Patient: That's helpful. I'll keep the number handy instead of sending another request through the website.
A trip is not a refill allowance

Fictional patient leaves Friday; record review is pending and pharmacist Ravi is available.

  1. Patient: I'm leaving Friday and would like to collect earlier than usual. Can you arrange that?
  2. Technician: I can pass the timing request to Ravi, our pharmacist. An earlier supply isn't confirmed.
  3. Patient: The label mentions refills remaining. Doesn't that mean any collection date is available to me?
  4. Technician: It shows a refill allowance, but it doesn't settle every timing or claim requirement.
  5. Patient: Could you tell Ravi I leave Friday morning, rather than saying I'm travelling soon?
  6. Technician: Yes, Friday morning is the useful detail. When would you be available for a call?
  7. Patient: Tomorrow afternoon is best. I don't need a promise, just an explanation of the options.
  8. Technician: I'll include that availability and ask Ravi to review the timing question with you.
  9. Patient: Should I make another refill request tonight if I haven't heard back by then?
  10. Technician: We already have your request. I'll add the travel details to it instead of creating another.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

A status call is not a promise of supply
  1. Read the renewal call with nine, eleven, and the three-to-four update window clearly distinguished.
  2. Repeat the transfer using 10:30, 12:30, and 16:00 to 17:00; do not turn its call into a collection promise.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Explain a later renewal update window

No refills remain. A request sent at 10:30 has no reply at 12:30. Technician Mei owns a status call between 16:00 and 17:00. No collection time is confirmed.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Technician: "The request was sent at ___."

Answer and explanation

A. 10:30

The supplied sending time is 10:30, not the current inquiry time.

2. Patient: "The current status at 12:30 is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. no reply

No prescriber reply has arrived at the stated current time.

3. Technician: "Mei will call between ___."

Answer and explanation

A. 16:00 and 17:00

That is the agreed communication window, not a collection guarantee.

4. Patient: "The collection time remains ___."

Answer and explanation

D. unconfirmed

The brief supplies no confirmed collection time despite the promised status call.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

REFERENCE
Course: Pharmacy Technician English
Professional audience: Pharmacy technicians and pharmacy support staff
Scope: All cases, calculations, and product limits are fictional language exercises, not patient-specific directions or clinical certification. Follow local law, authorized duties, pharmacist direction, privacy procedures, and actual recall notices. Urgent concerns need the appropriate prompt response, not a routine update window. US and UK sources provide context, not interchangeable rules. This book does not teach prescribing, dose selection, or clinical counseling.
Lesson 03: Refill requests and response windows
Original fictional case: At 11:00, patient Casey asks technician Jo about a refill. The record shows no refills remaining. A renewal request was sent to the prescriber at 09:00, but no response has arrived. Jo can call Casey between 15:00 and 16:00 with a status update. No prescriber response time, supply, or collection time is confirmed. Jo needs to explain what was sent, what remains pending, and what the call will cover. Any concern about running out or interrupted treatment should be referred promptly to the pharmacist rather than treated as safe to wait for the update.
Language workshop: A status call is not a promise of supply
Communication goal: Explain an unresolved renewal and make a reliable communication commitment without promising prescriber response or medicine availability.
Language guidance: A transmission confirmation may show that the request left the system or reached a destination. It is not a prescriber approval. Similarly, a status call between 15:00 and 16:00 is a communication commitment, not a guaranteed collection window.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): The record shows no refills remaining. / We sent the renewal request at nine this morning. / As of eleven, no response has arrived. / Sending the request does not mean the renewal is approved. / I will call you with the status between three and four. / That is an update window, not a confirmed collection time. / I cannot guarantee when the prescriber will respond. / No further supply has been confirmed yet. / Please discuss any concern about running out with the pharmacist now. / I cannot tell you to stop or change treatment while waiting. / I will tell you whether a response has arrived and what remains pending. / If I cannot reach you, I will record the attempt through our process. / Let us confirm the appropriate contact details. / No response is not the same as a refusal. / I am responsible for the agreed status call. / The request is pending, and I will update you even if it is still unresolved.
Vocabulary: refill: A further supply under an existing prescription when permitted and available. / refills remaining: The number of further supplies recorded as authorized under the prescription. / renewal: A request or authorization for a further prescription when the existing authorization is insufficient. / renewal request: A message asking the prescriber to authorize the relevant further prescription. / prescriber response: The actual reply from the prescribing professional or practice. / authorization pending: A status in which the required approval has not yet been received. / request timestamp: The time recorded for sending or receiving the request. / transmission confirmation: Evidence that a message was sent or delivered through a system, not that it was approved. / response window: A stated period for an expected or promised response whose status must be clear. / callback window: The agreed period in which a return call will be made. / status call: A call providing current information rather than guaranteeing an outcome. / collection time: The time at which an order is confirmed available for pickup. / supply authorization: The permission needed for the particular medicine supply under applicable rules. / partial fill: Supply of less than the prescribed quantity where permitted under applicable arrangements. / emergency supply: A medicine supply considered under specific legal and clinical requirements. / prescription transfer: Movement of prescription information between pharmacies where permitted. / refill too soon: A processing response indicating that the requested supply is earlier than the applicable rules allow. / remaining quantity: The amount still available or authorized under the relevant record. / adherence: Taking medicine in accordance with the agreed clinical instructions. / treatment interruption: A break in the prescribed treatment that may require clinical advice. / continuity of therapy: Maintaining appropriate treatment under qualified guidance. / follow-up owner: The person responsible for the specified next contact or action. / contact outcome: Whether and how a communication attempt reached the intended person. / pending response: A reply still awaited rather than received.
Speaking task: Read the renewal call with nine, eleven, and the three-to-four update window clearly distinguished. Repeat the transfer using 10:30, 12:30, and 16:00 to 17:00; do not turn its call into a collection promise. Check all four answers, then repeat the prompt pharmacist referral for a concern about running out.
Partner: Casey: Patient / Jo: Pharmacy technician
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: No refills remain. A request sent at 10:30 has no reply at 12:30. Technician Mei owns a status call between 16:00 and 17:00. No collection time is confirmed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Casey: Please use my verified contact details for the update. If you cannot reach me, I would not want the note to say I was fully informed.
Jo: I will record the actual contact outcome. A call attempt is not the same as speaking with you, and any further action must follow our communication process.
Casey: Thank you. I understand the renewal is still pending, no supply time is confirmed, and you will update me between three and four.
Jo: Correct. I am the follow-up owner for that call. We will keep your immediate concern with the pharmacist and keep the later update separate from any promise of prescriber response or medicine availability.
END REFERENCE
Practice grammar in context — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Optional second-round challenge: No refills remain. A request sent at 10:30 has no reply at 12:30. Technician Mei owns a status call between 16:00 and 17:00. No collection time is confirmed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Casey: Please use my verified contact details for the update. If you cannot reach me, I would not want the note to say I was fully informed.
Jo: I will record the actual contact outcome. A call attempt is not the same as speaking with you, and any further action must follow our communication process.
Casey: Thank you. I understand the renewal is still pending, no supply time is confirmed, and you will update me between three and four.
Jo: Correct. I am the follow-up owner for that call. We will keep your immediate concern with the pharmacist and keep the later update separate from any promise of prescriber response or medicine availability.
END REFERENCE
04Stock enquiries and delivery estimatesWednesday is an estimate, not a collection promise+

Your goal

Explain a stock shortage, qualify a supplier forecast, and route alternative-treatment questions without choosing a substitute.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

Wednesday is an estimate, not a collection promise

The pharmacy has no stock for patient Robin's order. The wholesaler estimates delivery on Wednesday, but shipment has not been confirmed. Technician Lee can check the next supplier update on Tuesday at 16:00. The pharmacist handles questions about alternatives or immediate clinical need. Lee must separate no stock now, a future delivery estimate, dispatch confirmation, actual receipt, and readiness for collection. No exact arrival hour, guaranteed quantity, substitute medicine, or patient collection time is established. A forecast must not become a clinical instruction to wait.

Robin: Patient / Lee: Pharmacy technician

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the current local stock position?

Answer and explanation

C. No stock for this order

The brief states that the pharmacy has no stock for the patient's order.

2. What does Wednesday describe?

Answer and explanation

A. The wholesaler's unconfirmed delivery estimate

Wednesday is a forecast from the supplier, with shipment still unconfirmed.

3. When can Lee check the supplier update?

Answer and explanation

A. Tuesday at 16:00

The stated next information checkpoint is Tuesday at 16:00.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

No stock at one pharmacy does not by itself prove a regional or national shortage. Likewise, an expected delivery day does not confirm dispatch, the full requested quantity, pharmacy receipt, or collection readiness. Name the status actually known.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
stock on hand
Product physically held at the location under the relevant inventory record.verify stock on hand
available stock
Stock actually available for the intended use after relevant restrictions and checks.confirm available stock
stockout
A situation in which the required product is not available in stock.report a stockout
backorder
An order awaiting stock rather than immediately fulfilled.track a backorder
wholesaler
A business supplying products to pharmacies or other authorized customers.contact the wholesaler
supplier estimate
A forecast given by the supplying organization.qualify the supplier estimate
shipment confirmation
Verification that the relevant shipment has actually been arranged or sent as specified.obtain shipment confirmation
dispatch
Sending goods from the supplier or distribution point.confirm dispatch
carrier tracking
Recorded information about movement of a shipment.check carrier tracking
receipt
Actual arrival of the relevant goods at the destination.confirm receipt
delivery window
The period in which a delivery is expected or committed, with status specified.clarify the delivery window
allocation
A quantity assigned to a particular order or customer.verify the allocation
lead time
The period between an order or request and the relevant delivery or completion.qualify the lead time
supply disruption
An interruption affecting the availability or movement of a product.report a supply disruption
regional shortage
Limited availability across a defined area rather than one pharmacy alone.verify a regional shortage
product availability
Whether a specified product can actually be obtained under the relevant conditions.confirm product availability
generic substitution
Supply of an eligible equivalent generic product under applicable rules and authority.refer a generic-substitution question
therapeutic alternative
A different treatment option requiring appropriate clinical consideration.discuss a therapeutic alternative
interchangeability
Whether products may replace one another under relevant clinical and legal requirements.verify interchangeability
order allocation
The part of stock or supply assigned to the particular order.check order allocation
supplier checkpoint
An agreed time to obtain updated supplier information.confirm the supplier checkpoint
collection readiness
Confirmation that the order has completed the relevant steps for pickup.verify collection readiness
partial delivery
Receipt of less than the ordered quantity.identify a partial delivery
unconfirmed forecast
An expected outcome that has not been established as a commitment or event.label an unconfirmed forecast

A possible alternative is not automatically interchangeable with the prescribed product. Route clinical and substitution questions to the pharmacist under actual rules. The supplier checkpoint is a communication step, not a recommendation about whether the person can safely wait.

C Notice the language

A useful estimate includes its source and limits

A patient may turn Wednesday into I can collect Wednesday morning. Keep the timing qualification attached each time it is repeated. Explain what will be checked and where the patient can raise an immediate need instead of giving vague reassurance.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
State current availability
We do not currently have stock for your order.
Attribute the estimate
The wholesaler estimates Wednesday delivery.
Qualify dispatch
Shipment has not yet been confirmed.
Avoid a collection promise
I cannot confirm a pickup time from that estimate alone.
Name the checkpoint
I can check the supplier update Tuesday at sixteen hundred.
Separate stages
Dispatch, receipt, and readiness for collection are different statuses.
Avoid overstating shortage
This confirms our local stock position, not availability everywhere.
Check quantity
We need the quantity for this order confirmed as well as timing.
Route alternatives
The pharmacist can discuss appropriate alternatives with you.
Do not substitute independently
I cannot choose another medicine from the stock shelf.
Address immediate need
Please tell the pharmacist about any concern that needs prompt attention.
Preserve the uncertainty
Wednesday remains an estimate, not a guarantee.
Request a useful supplier update
Has this order been allocated and dispatched, or is it still awaiting confirmation?
Keep receipt factual
A tracking movement does not mean the pharmacy has received the product.
Explain remaining work
Receipt still needs the relevant pharmacy checks before collection is confirmed.
Close the message
No stock now; Wednesday estimated; shipment unconfirmed; supplier check Tuesday at four.
6 language and precision notes
Currently
Describes the present stock position without making it permanent.
The wholesaler estimates
Identifies the source and forecast status.
From that estimate alone
Explains why collection timing remains uncertain.
For this order
Prevents a general supply update from being mistaken for allocated stock.
Appropriate alternatives
Leaves clinical suitability and legal substitution with the pharmacist.
Still needs
Makes later required steps visible without inventing their completion time.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which statement preserves the facts?

Answer and explanation

A. We have no stock; Wednesday is estimated, shipment is unconfirmed, and Lee will check Tuesday at 16:00.

The statement separates the current shortage, supplier forecast, shipment gap, and information checkpoint.

2. What does carrier movement not establish by itself?

Answer and explanation

D. Collection readiness at the pharmacy

Transport progress does not establish receipt and completion of the remaining pharmacy steps.

3. Who should address an alternative medicine question?

Answer and explanation

D. The pharmacist under the applicable process

Alternative treatment or substitution requires the appropriate professional and legal process.

4. What does Tuesday at 16:00 represent?

Answer and explanation

C. A supplier information check, not guaranteed supply or clinical advice to wait

The brief gives a checkpoint for information and does not establish a supply outcome or safe waiting period.

Check explanations in the book, page 91 ↗

E Conversations

Wednesday is an estimate, not a collection promise

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Robin: Patient / Lee: Pharmacy technician

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • supplier checkpoint
  • order allocation
  • therapeutic alternative
  • stockout
  • supplier estimate
  • partial delivery
  • Collection readiness
  • regional shortage
  • Carrier tracking
  • shipment confirmation
  1. Robin
    Lee, I was told there was no stock for my order. Has that changed, or are you still waiting for a delivery?
  2. Lee
    We still have a 1 for this order. The wholesaler estimates Wednesday delivery, but shipment is not confirmed, so I cannot tell you the order is available now.
  3. Robin
    I can get a lift Wednesday morning. Should I book it, or does Wednesday just mean that is when the supplier hopes to deliver?
  4. Lee
    No. It is a 2, without a confirmed hour or shipment. I should not turn that into a Wednesday-morning collection promise when the information does not establish one.
  5. Robin
    Does no shipment confirmation mean the supplier definitely cannot provide it, or only that you do not yet have confirmation?
  6. Lee
    We do not yet have 3. That is different from a confirmed cancellation. I will keep the uncertainty clear rather than replace it with either a guarantee or an unsupported refusal.
  7. Robin
    When will you next be able to check? I do not want to call repeatedly if there is a particular update point.
  8. Lee
    The 4 is Tuesday at sixteen hundred. I can check the latest status then, including whether the order has been allocated, dispatched, or remains unconfirmed.
  9. Robin
    If the tracking page starts showing movement, would that be enough for you to tell me I can collect it?
  10. Lee
    5 may show progress in transit, but it is not the same as pharmacy receipt. The actual product and order status still need to be verified.
  11. Robin
    Is my whole quantity included? Last time a delivery arrived, but only part of what I needed was in it.
  12. Lee
    Correct. We need the 6 confirmed for your order. A shipment to the pharmacy does not automatically prove that the requested quantity is included or complete.
  13. Robin
    If only some arrives, I suppose that is another question rather than a reason to tell me the entire order has been fulfilled.
  14. Lee
    Yes. A 7 should be described accurately. Any decision about what may be supplied to you must follow the pharmacist's process and applicable requirements, not my assumption.
  15. Robin
    What about a different medicine? I would like to discuss options if waiting is a problem, but I do not want a substitute chosen just because it is on the shelf.
  16. Lee
    The pharmacist can discuss a 8 or other appropriate route. Please raise any immediate need with them now; the delivery estimate is not advice that you should interrupt treatment or wait without guidance.
  17. Robin
    Does your stock problem mean the product is unavailable across the region? That would affect how I understand the situation.
  18. Lee
    We have not established a 9. The fact I can confirm is our current local stock position. A broader claim needs its own verified information.
  19. Robin
    Thank you. I will not treat Wednesday as a pickup appointment. Please keep the supplier's estimate and any later confirmed facts separate.
  20. Lee
    I will. 10 comes after the appropriate steps are confirmed. For now: no stock, Wednesday estimated, shipment unconfirmed, and the supplier checkpoint Tuesday at four.

Answers and explanations
  1. stockout Turn 2. The stockout describes the pharmacy's current lack of the required product, not a confirmed shortage at every pharmacy.
  2. supplier estimate Turn 4. The supplier estimate forecasts Wednesday delivery without guaranteeing a morning arrival or a patient collection time.
  3. shipment confirmation Turn 6. Shipment confirmation has not arrived; that gap is not itself a definite cancellation or completed dispatch.
  4. supplier checkpoint Turn 8. The supplier checkpoint is Tuesday at sixteen hundred, when Lee can check updated information rather than guarantee delivery.
  5. Carrier tracking Turn 10. Carrier tracking records shipment movement and does not establish that the product has reached the pharmacy or completed its checks.
  6. order allocation Turn 12. Order allocation identifies the quantity assigned to this particular order rather than a general delivery or stock announcement.
  7. partial delivery Turn 14. A partial delivery contains less than the ordered quantity and should not be reported as complete fulfillment.
  8. therapeutic alternative Turn 16. A therapeutic alternative is a clinical option requiring appropriate assessment rather than a technician's stock-based replacement choice.
  9. regional shortage Turn 18. A regional shortage concerns availability across an area and cannot be inferred solely from this pharmacy's local stockout.
  10. Collection readiness Turn 20. Collection readiness requires the relevant receipt and pharmacy checks, not just a supplier forecast or movement in transit.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Robin: Lee, I was told there was no stock for my order. Has that changed, or are you still waiting for a delivery?
  2. Lee: We still have a stockout for this order. The wholesaler estimates Wednesday delivery, but shipment is not confirmed, so I cannot tell you the order is available now.
  3. Robin: I can get a lift Wednesday morning. Should I book it, or does Wednesday just mean that is when the supplier hopes to deliver?
  4. Lee: No. It is a supplier estimate, without a confirmed hour or shipment. I should not turn that into a Wednesday-morning collection promise when the information does not establish one.
  5. Robin: Does no shipment confirmation mean the supplier definitely cannot provide it, or only that you do not yet have confirmation?
  6. Lee: We do not yet have shipment confirmation. That is different from a confirmed cancellation. I will keep the uncertainty clear rather than replace it with either a guarantee or an unsupported refusal.
  7. Robin: When will you next be able to check? I do not want to call repeatedly if there is a particular update point.
  8. Lee: The supplier checkpoint is Tuesday at sixteen hundred. I can check the latest status then, including whether the order has been allocated, dispatched, or remains unconfirmed.
  9. Robin: If the tracking page starts showing movement, would that be enough for you to tell me I can collect it?
  10. Lee: Carrier tracking may show progress in transit, but it is not the same as pharmacy receipt. The actual product and order status still need to be verified.
  11. Robin: Is my whole quantity included? Last time a delivery arrived, but only part of what I needed was in it.
  12. Lee: Correct. We need the order allocation confirmed for your order. A shipment to the pharmacy does not automatically prove that the requested quantity is included or complete.
  13. Robin: If only some arrives, I suppose that is another question rather than a reason to tell me the entire order has been fulfilled.
  14. Lee: Yes. A partial delivery should be described accurately. Any decision about what may be supplied to you must follow the pharmacist's process and applicable requirements, not my assumption.
  15. Robin: What about a different medicine? I would like to discuss options if waiting is a problem, but I do not want a substitute chosen just because it is on the shelf.
  16. Lee: The pharmacist can discuss a therapeutic alternative or other appropriate route. Please raise any immediate need with them now; the delivery estimate is not advice that you should interrupt treatment or wait without guidance.
  17. Robin: Does your stock problem mean the product is unavailable across the region? That would affect how I understand the situation.
  18. Lee: We have not established a regional shortage. The fact I can confirm is our current local stock position. A broader claim needs its own verified information.
  19. Robin: Thank you. I will not treat Wednesday as a pickup appointment. Please keep the supplier's estimate and any later confirmed facts separate.
  20. Lee: I will. Collection readiness comes after the appropriate steps are confirmed. For now: no stock, Wednesday estimated, shipment unconfirmed, and the supplier checkpoint Tuesday at four.
Dialogue in the book, page 37 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Wednesday is an estimate

Fictional stock enquiry using the lesson case.

  1. Patient: Someone mentioned Wednesday. Should I book a taxi now to collect my prescription then?
  2. Technician: Wednesday is the estimated arrival from our wholesaler. We have no stock and no shipment confirmation.
  3. Patient: So the wholesaler hasn't actually told you the package is on its way?
  4. Technician: That's correct. Lee will check the supplier's update Tuesday at four, when more information may appear.
  5. Patient: Could you call Wednesday a collection date if I promise to come late?
  6. Technician: No, coming later doesn't turn an estimate into confirmed arrival or prescription readiness.
  7. Patient: I'd like to discuss what the wait means for me, beyond the delivery question.
  8. Technician: I'll connect you with the pharmacist for that. I can't advise on medication choices myself.
  9. Patient: Please keep the stock update separate so I know whether to arrange transport.
  10. Technician: I will. For now, the taxi would be based on an unconfirmed delivery estimate.
Six assigned from ten requested

Fictional pharmacy supply call about a fictional nonclinical stock order; no patient allocation decisions.

  1. Technician: Our order lists ten units, but your update shows six. Which quantity is actually assigned?
  2. Wholesaler representative: Six units are allocated. The other four remain on backorder with no arrival estimate.
  3. Technician: Does six allocated mean six dispatched, or are they still awaiting your shipping confirmation?
  4. Wholesaler representative: They haven't dispatched yet. Allocation is confirmed; dispatch time isn't available on my screen.
  5. Technician: Please give me the order reference so our stock lead can match this update.
  6. Wholesaler representative: It's order W83. The six assigned units and four outstanding units are listed separately.
  7. Technician: I'll report those quantities separately. We don't have a patient collection promise attached to them.
  8. Wholesaler representative: That's helpful. A shipping notice will identify what actually leaves, not just what was requested.
  9. Technician: Could your next update retain that split rather than saying the whole order is progressing?
  10. Wholesaler representative: Yes. I'll keep the allocated quantity, outstanding quantity, and shipment status explicit for W83.
The branch display is not a reservation

Fictional customer sees online stock at another branch; no availability has been checked directly.

  1. Patient: Your website shows stock at the Riverside branch. Does that mean they're holding it for me?
  2. Technician: No, that display isn't a reservation. We haven't confirmed their current stock or your order arrangements.
  3. Patient: Could you check whether I should travel there? It's forty minutes each way.
  4. Technician: I can ask their pharmacy team about the stock enquiry and explain the travel concern.
  5. Patient: Would checking stock also move my prescription automatically from this branch to theirs?
  6. Technician: No. A stock enquiry and a prescription transfer are separate matters for the pharmacy teams.
  7. Patient: Then please ask about availability without cancelling anything here or promising them my collection.
  8. Technician: That's clear. I'll describe this as an availability enquiry, with no transfer confirmed.
  9. Patient: Can the pharmacist explain what options I have if the display is out of date?
  10. Technician: Yes. I'll include that question when I pass on the enquiry and the travel detail.

Vocabulary: stock on hand: Product physically held at the location under the relevant inventory record. / available stock: Stock actually available for the intended use after relevant restrictions and checks. / stockout: A situation in which the required product is not available in stock. / backorder: An order awaiting stock rather than immediately fulfilled. / wholesaler: A business supplying products to pharmacies or other authorized customers. / supplier estimate: A forecast given by the supplying organization. / shipment confirmation: Verification that the relevant shipment has actually been arranged or sent as specified. / dispatch: Sending goods from the supplier or distribution point. / carrier tracking: Recorded information about movement of a shipment. / receipt: Actual arrival of the relevant goods at the destination. / delivery window: The period in which a delivery is expected or committed, with status specified. / allocation: A quantity assigned to a particular order or customer. / lead time: The period between an order or request and the relevant delivery or completion. / supply disruption: An interruption affecting the availability or movement of a product. / regional shortage: Limited availability across a defined area rather than one pharmacy alone. / product availability: Whether a specified product can actually be obtained under the relevant conditions. / generic substitution: Supply of an eligible equivalent generic product under applicable rules and authority. / therapeutic alternative: A different treatment option requiring appropriate clinical consideration. / interchangeability: Whether products may replace one another under relevant clinical and legal requirements. / order allocation: The part of stock or supply assigned to the particular order. / supplier checkpoint: An agreed time to obtain updated supplier information. / collection readiness: Confirmation that the order has completed the relevant steps for pickup. / partial delivery: Receipt of less than the ordered quantity. / unconfirmed forecast: An expected outcome that has not been established as a commitment or event.
Speaking task: Read the supplier-status exchange aloud, stressing estimated rather than guaranteed. Complete the Friday transfer with Thursday at 11:00 as the information checkpoint. Check the key, then repeat the distinction between dispatch, receipt, and collection readiness.
Partner: Robin: Patient / Lee: Pharmacy technician
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: No stock is available for an order. The supplier estimates Friday delivery, but dispatch is unconfirmed. Technician Bea can check Thursday at 11:00. The pharmacist handles alternative-treatment questions.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Robin: Does your stock problem mean the product is unavailable across the region? That would affect how I understand the situation.
Lee: We have not established a regional shortage. The fact I can confirm is our current local stock position. A broader claim needs its own verified information.
Robin: Thank you. I will not treat Wednesday as a pickup appointment. Please keep the supplier's estimate and any later confirmed facts separate.
Lee: I will. Collection readiness comes after the appropriate steps are confirmed. For now: no stock, Wednesday estimated, shipment unconfirmed, and the supplier checkpoint Tuesday at four.
END REFERENCE
Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

Professional audience: Pharmacy technicians and pharmacy support staff
Scope: All cases, calculations, and product limits are fictional language exercises, not patient-specific directions or clinical certification. Follow local law, authorized duties, pharmacist direction, privacy procedures, and actual recall notices. Urgent concerns need the appropriate prompt response, not a routine update window. US and UK sources provide context, not interchangeable rules. This book does not teach prescribing, dose selection, or clinical counseling.
Lesson 04: Stock enquiries and delivery estimates

05Pickup questions and pharmacist consultationsThe label question has a named pharmacist+

Your goal

Recognize an auxiliary-label question and complete a clear pharmacist handoff without interpreting or changing the instruction.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

The label question has a named pharmacist

At pickup, patient Arden asks technician Kit what an auxiliary label means. Kit can identify the label but is not authorized to interpret its instruction. Pharmacist Ravi is available at the consultation counter and has accepted the question. No instructions are changed in this scenario, and the actual label wording is not supplied as a clinical direction. Kit should acknowledge the uncertainty, explain the handoff, and help Arden reach Ravi without implying that a label is optional or that handing over the package has already answered the question.

Arden: Patient / Kit: Pharmacy technician

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What does Arden ask about?

Answer and explanation

D. An auxiliary label

The question concerns the meaning of an additional label on the medicine package.

2. Who has accepted the question?

Answer and explanation

C. Pharmacist Ravi

Ravi is available at the consultation counter and has accepted the handoff.

3. What changes to the instructions are established?

Answer and explanation

B. None

The scenario explicitly states that no instructions are changed.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

An auxiliary label adds information; it is not optional merely because it is separate from the main label. The technician can identify which label prompted the question while the pharmacist explains its meaning for the actual medicine and patient.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
auxiliary label
An additional label providing a relevant medicine instruction or caution.identify the auxiliary label
dispensing label
The pharmacy label with the relevant patient and medicine directions.verify the dispensing label
patient counseling
A professional discussion helping the patient understand the medicine and its use.arrange patient counseling
consultation counter
The designated place for the pharmacist discussion.direct to the consultation counter
medication guide
Patient information supplied for certain medicines under relevant requirements.provide the medication guide
patient information leaflet
Written product information intended for the patient.identify the patient information leaflet
package insert
Product information supplied with the medicine, with audience and purpose varying by format.identify the package insert
warning
Information drawing attention to a relevant risk or required caution.clarify a warning
precaution
A condition or measure requiring care in using a medicine.ask about a precaution
contraindication
A condition in which a medicine or treatment should not be used under its labeling or clinical assessment.refer a contraindication question
interaction
A relevant effect arising from a medicine used with another substance or condition.ask about an interaction
food interaction
A possible effect involving medicine use and food.refer a food-interaction question
alcohol interaction
A possible effect involving medicine use and alcohol.refer an alcohol-interaction question
drowsiness
A tendency to feel sleepy or less alert.report drowsiness
storage condition
A requirement for keeping a product appropriately.clarify storage conditions
refrigeration
Storage under specified refrigerated conditions when required.clarify a refrigeration instruction
light protection
Storage or handling intended to limit relevant light exposure.clarify light-protection wording
measuring device
A device intended to measure an appropriate quantity accurately.identify the supplied measuring device
administration device
Equipment used to deliver a medicine under the specified directions.identify the administration device
privacy
Protection of personal information and conversation from inappropriate exposure.protect privacy
teach-back
A check in which the person explains their understanding so the professional can clarify it.use teach-back appropriately
accepted handoff
A transfer of a question or task acknowledged by the receiving person.confirm an accepted handoff
instruction interpretation
Explanation of what a direction means for the person's medicine use.refer instruction interpretation
counseling completion
Confirmation that the actual professional discussion occurred, not merely that it was offered.record counseling completion

Offered counseling, accepted handoff, and completed counseling describe different events. Ravi accepting the question gives it an owner. It does not prove that the discussion has happened, that the person understands, or that any instruction has changed.

C Notice the language

A question at pickup is part of the visit

A queue can make someone reluctant to ask about medicine instructions. Acknowledge the question without rushing the person or suggesting that familiar wording is obvious. A named pharmacist handoff is more helpful than pointing vaguely toward another counter.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Welcome the question
Thank you for asking about that label.
Identify the item
You are referring to the auxiliary label beside the main dispensing label.
State the boundary
I can identify it, but I cannot interpret the instruction for you.
Name the pharmacist
Ravi has accepted your question.
Give a clear location
Ravi is available at the consultation counter.
Keep the wording intact
I will pass on the exact label and your question.
Avoid an optionality claim
An additional label is not automatically optional.
Do not change instructions
I am not changing any direction on this package.
Explain the handoff
The pharmacist will discuss what the instruction means for your medicine.
Respect privacy
We can use the practice's appropriate private discussion arrangement.
Clarify the uncertainty
Please tell Ravi which part is unclear.
Invite checking understanding
You can explain what you understood and ask Ravi to clarify it.
Avoid a false completion
The question is accepted; the consultation has not yet been completed.
Route device questions
Ravi can address the appropriate use of the supplied device within the consultation.
Keep pickup and counseling distinct
Handing over a package does not itself answer a clinical question.
Close with the owner
I will connect you with Ravi, who has accepted the question.
6 language and precision notes
Thank you for asking
Makes clarification welcome rather than an interruption to the queue.
Beside the main label
Identifies the physical source without interpreting it.
Has accepted
Gives the question a confirmed professional owner.
Not automatically optional
Corrects an inference without supplying a clinical instruction.
Explain what you understood
Supports clarification instead of testing the patient's intelligence.
Not yet completed
Keeps the handoff distinct from the actual consultation.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which response completes the appropriate handoff?

Answer and explanation

A. Ravi has accepted your auxiliary-label question and is available at the consultation counter.

The response identifies the question, confirmed pharmacist owner, and practical location without changing the instruction.

2. What does Ravi's acceptance establish?

Answer and explanation

D. The question has a receiving pharmacist, not a completed consultation.

Acceptance confirms the handoff while the actual discussion and understanding remain separate.

3. Which statement is inappropriate?

Answer and explanation

C. The auxiliary label is always optional.

An additional label can contain important directions or cautions and cannot be dismissed as optional by category.

4. What should the patient be encouraged to clarify?

Answer and explanation

D. The actual unclear wording and their understanding during the pharmacist discussion

The pharmacist needs the real instruction and question to provide an appropriate explanation.

E Conversations

The label question has a named pharmacist

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Arden: Patient / Kit: Pharmacy technician

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • counseling completion
  • dispensing label
  • auxiliary label
  • patient information leaflet
  • administration device
  • patient counseling
  • accepted handoff
  • teach-back
  • privacy
  • consultation counter
  1. Arden
    Kit, before I finish collecting this, what does the extra label mean? I recognize the words, but I am not sure how they apply to me.
  2. Kit
    Thank you for asking. You are referring to the 1 beside the main label. I can identify it, but pharmacist Ravi needs to explain the instruction for your medicine.
  3. Arden
    I assumed that if it was important, everything would be on the main label. Is an extra sticker just general advice I can ignore?
  4. Kit
    It is not automatically optional. Ravi can explain how the 2 and additional information apply together. A label's position on the package is not a reason to disregard it.
  5. Arden
    Could you just tell me quickly? Everyone behind me is waiting, and I feel awkward asking for another conversation.
  6. Kit
    Your question deserves proper 3. Ravi has accepted it and is available. I will connect you with him; I am not authorized to interpret the instruction.
  7. Arden
    Where should I go? I want to know the person is expecting me and understands the question.
  8. Kit
    Ravi is at the 4. I will make clear that your question is about this auxiliary label, so the handoff includes the issue rather than only your arrival.
  9. Arden
    That helps. Does accepting the question mean somebody has already checked everything with me, or simply that Ravi is going to discuss it?
  10. Kit
    It is an 5, not a completed discussion. We should not record counseling as finished just because the question was passed on or a package reached the pickup counter.
  11. Arden
    The leaflet says it differently. Can I show Ravi both? I am not sure whether they mean the same thing.
  12. Kit
    Bring the 6 into the consultation question. Ravi can address the actual wording and context; I should not resolve an apparent difference by choosing an instruction for you.
  13. Arden
    There is a device with the package too. Could I ask about it in the pharmacist conversation?
  14. Kit
    Yes. A question about the 7 belongs in the appropriate consultation. I will not assume that recognizing the package means you have already been shown or understand the device.
  15. Arden
    I would prefer to ask the more personal part somewhere people in the queue cannot easily hear. Is that possible through the pharmacy's arrangements?
  16. Kit
    We can protect your 8. I will tell Ravi that you would like a private discussion without announcing your question to everyone here.
  17. Arden
    Once Ravi explains it, I may need to repeat what I understood. Sometimes I say yes because I heard the words, but the meaning is still unclear.
  18. Kit
    That kind of 9 can help Ravi clarify the explanation. You can say what you understood and ask about anything that does not fit, rather than feel obliged to pretend.
  19. Arden
    Thank you. Please keep the original directions unchanged and connect me with Ravi. I want the meaning clarified, not a new instruction guessed at this counter.
  20. Kit
    No direction has been changed. We will distinguish the handoff from 10, and Ravi will address the question through the consultation. I will preserve the label reference and your request for a clear explanation.

Answers and explanations
  1. auxiliary label Turn 2. The auxiliary label is the additional label prompting the question; identifying it is different from interpreting its instruction.
  2. dispensing label Turn 4. The dispensing label and additional information may have different functions; the main label does not make every auxiliary instruction optional.
  3. patient counseling Turn 6. Patient counseling is the professional discussion needed to understand the instruction, not a hurried interpretation by an unauthorized technician.
  4. consultation counter Turn 8. The consultation counter gives Arden a specific location for the pharmacist who has accepted the question.
  5. accepted handoff Turn 10. An accepted handoff confirms that Ravi will receive the question, not that the counseling discussion has already occurred.
  6. patient information leaflet Turn 12. The patient information leaflet is another information source the pharmacist can discuss in relation to the actual label and question.
  7. administration device Turn 14. An administration device relates to delivering the medicine and should be discussed within the appropriate pharmacist consultation and role.
  8. privacy Turn 16. Privacy means handling the personal question through appropriate discussion arrangements rather than exposing it to the queue.
  9. teach-back Turn 18. Teach-back checks the person's understanding so the professional can clarify the explanation; it is not proof of understanding from a simple yes.
  10. counseling completion Turn 20. Counseling completion concerns the actual professional discussion, which must not be recorded merely because the referral or package handover occurred.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Arden: Kit, before I finish collecting this, what does the extra label mean? I recognize the words, but I am not sure how they apply to me.
  2. Kit: Thank you for asking. You are referring to the auxiliary label beside the main label. I can identify it, but pharmacist Ravi needs to explain the instruction for your medicine.
  3. Arden: I assumed that if it was important, everything would be on the main label. Is an extra sticker just general advice I can ignore?
  4. Kit: It is not automatically optional. Ravi can explain how the dispensing label and additional information apply together. A label's position on the package is not a reason to disregard it.
  5. Arden: Could you just tell me quickly? Everyone behind me is waiting, and I feel awkward asking for another conversation.
  6. Kit: Your question deserves proper patient counseling. Ravi has accepted it and is available. I will connect you with him; I am not authorized to interpret the instruction.
  7. Arden: Where should I go? I want to know the person is expecting me and understands the question.
  8. Kit: Ravi is at the consultation counter. I will make clear that your question is about this auxiliary label, so the handoff includes the issue rather than only your arrival.
  9. Arden: That helps. Does accepting the question mean somebody has already checked everything with me, or simply that Ravi is going to discuss it?
  10. Kit: It is an accepted handoff, not a completed discussion. We should not record counseling as finished just because the question was passed on or a package reached the pickup counter.
  11. Arden: The leaflet says it differently. Can I show Ravi both? I am not sure whether they mean the same thing.
  12. Kit: Bring the patient information leaflet into the consultation question. Ravi can address the actual wording and context; I should not resolve an apparent difference by choosing an instruction for you.
  13. Arden: There is a device with the package too. Could I ask about it in the pharmacist conversation?

  15. Arden: I would prefer to ask the more personal part somewhere people in the queue cannot easily hear. Is that possible through the pharmacy's arrangements?
  16. Kit: We can protect your privacy. I will tell Ravi that you would like a private discussion without announcing your question to everyone here.
